Checks 2 and 3 — the visible summary table and the section headers, the whole #322 hardening that exists because a hand-typed board once read 🩷45/🔴126/Σ294 — **never ran**, check 1's verdict was **discarded**, and doc-lint reads only the exit code with stderr sent to `/dev/null`, so **the gate printed "doc-lint: OK — no drift" over a check doing nothing**. Fixed at the root: **falling out of `--check` is now itself exit 2**, and doc-lint separates *"the check could not run"* from *"the doc drifted"*. Proven four ways — broke the table (`doc=999 real=92`), broke a header on a synthetic copy (`doc=777 real=30`), re-introduced the exact regression (**exit 2, not 0**), and ran doc-lint against it. **Four for four this week** (#578 ×2, #579, #581): every one a check that looked green while doing nothing — and this one was **introduced by a fix, on the same day, and shipped past a green gate**, which is the argument for a structural guard over another careful edit. Minted #580, #581. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — all green.
- **26 Jul (📮 THE SHARED `FROM` IS GONE — option B locked, and the product can finally speak to a mailbox that isn't ours):** founder pushed back on my recommendation and was right to. I had said *"A, because deliverability is reputation and theirs is warm"* — **which is not an argument for A at all**: warming is a property of the MAILBOX, not of who presses send. Provider warms it either way. Once that fell away the real trade-off was visible, and it is the founder's own point: **we have already built the sending brain** — sequence engine, send window, daily caps, A/B subjects, kill-switch, reply classifier — and **A puts a second brain in charge of *when* an email goes out**, which you cannot have. **B locked.** B also earns what A never could: a client who says *"use my own Google Workspace"* is a client A has to turn away. Founder then sent the Zapmail/SmartSenders screen as proof — **$45 = domain $18/yr + 3 mailboxes at $9/mo, on real Google and Outlook**, not a black-box sender, which is what makes B buildable at all. **Built:** `lib/sending-inbox.ts` — the sender is resolved per client from `client_inboxes`, as a **pure decision function** (18 tests) so the ordering rules and every refusal path are provable without a database. Two rules it pins, both money: a **`warming` branded mailbox never sends** (sending on it is exactly what un-warms it), so during the SOP's pooled→branded overlap the **pooled** one sends; and a half-configured preferred mailbox is **skipped, not fatal**, so a blank branded row can't take a paying client off the air. `lib/mailer.ts` — nodemailer, one transport per send (a pooled connection shared across clients could put one client's mail down another's authenticated session), returning the **same verdict shape as `interpretSend`** so every #338 rollback path — delete the phantom row, roll the claim back, alert, leave it due — keeps working byte-for-byte. `lib/inbox-secret.ts` — AES-256-GCM, fresh IV per encryption, authenticated so a tampered value **throws** rather than handing garbage to a mail server and locking out a warmed mailbox; `describeCipher` is the only thing any surface may show (25 tests). **`figsy.ts:26` — the line this whole blocker was named after — is deleted**, and `COLD_FROM` is no longer imported by the cold path, so there is no fallback left to fall back TO. **Retired the #311 `RESEND_API_KEY` guard on both cold paths**: cold mail no longer touches Resend, so gating it on that key would have blocked every send on a credential the path stopped using — the concern behind #311 now sits one level lower, in a resolve that refuses loudly instead of writing a phantom row. **🧍 Owed: `INBOX_SECRET_KEY` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) and one mailbox's SMTP details.** ⚠️ Flagged: Google and Microsoft both block plain SMTP by default (App Password / SMTP AUTH per mailbox). Gates: api tsc clean · **466** vitest · doc-lint green. Migration `20260726_inbox_smtp` (additive, idempotent) is in the Engine card.
- **26 Jul (📚 ONE SOURCE OF TRUTH — LAUNCH-PAD rebuilt as the execution list, the fifth status doc retired, the sending spine minted as #547–#560):** founder, plainly: *"the docs we hold accountable are not right… the one source of truth is so wrong. launchpad."* He was right, and the reason had a name. **`BUILD-STATUS.md` was a fifth status doc** — which root `CLAUDE.md` forbids for exactly this failure mode — and its summary read *"The ONLY items not built: 🚩 #515 + ⏸ CI · Nothing left"* while **#211, the entire per-client sending spine, was 🔴 and a paying client could not be delivered at all**. It also rolled *"FIGSY sourcing · ICP builder · campaigns · sequences · inbox"* into one ✅ row, which reads as "sending works". Retired to `docs/archive/BUILD-STATUS-26JUL.md` with a banner naming what was wrong, not silently deleted (#555). **LAUNCH-PAD is now what he asked for:** one table per block — item · description · owner · mirrored dot — ordered **A→E**, no dates, opening with an HONEST STATE paragraph that says we cannot sell delivery today and names the line of code that is the reason (`figsy.ts:26 const FROM = COLD_FROM`). Everything that does not get us live **left the page with its new home named** — the expired 14-day map and the retired $1+$3-hold money walk to KIND-MASTER history, #515 and the whole Nexus family and Alta-parity to V2-TRACKER, legal and the ops-depth items to their own inventory rows. **#211 was re-verified in code line by line before writing any of it** (module-level `FROM`, `client_inboxes` read by no send path, no SMTP client in the dependency tree, `lib/smartlead.ts` a key-verification stub, Instantly not integrated, all replies through one Resend inbound webhook) and then **broken into buildable parts: #547 resolve the account's inbox or fail closed · #548 a transport that can speak AS a client mailbox (founder call: provider API vs SMTP-per-inbox) · #549 Instantly = ours, inside the product · #550 Smartlead = the clients' · #551 replies land against the right inbox · #552 inbox state on screen and gating the work · #553 the first-send ladder.** Four more were minted for things never done rather than left in prose: **#554 RLS never audited** (seven migration files unread; the anon key is public and #350 proved a policy shipped as `USING(true)` — the only true breach risk left), **#558 the repo's migrations no longer describe production** (the live `credit_transactions` CHECK was widened by hand, so re-running `20260603_schema_reconcile.sql` would break every wallet write), **#559 the nine screens walked against MBF**, **#560 shrink the surface** (~6% of 90k lines read; the retired trees are still reachable). **#556 makes `CASHFLOW-LAB.html` the money model of record**, referenced from all four docs, and **#557 stamps the four stale flow/preview docs** — the retired trial, the *"nothing to code"* outreach flow the founder overruled, the old $1/$3/$5/$6 ladder, and *"your own warmed inbox"* — as corrected rather than deleting the decision trail. Docs only, no code touched. Gates: `doc-lint.sh` + `update-board.sh` green.
- **26 Jul (🚫 A CONTROL THAT HID ITSELF — caught by the founder on the very next step):** the founder went to run the migrations and **the button wasn't there.** The Engine page's "Run it now" lived INSIDE the amber *"inbox tracking is waiting on one migration"* banner, which renders only when `migration_pending` is true — a flag derived purely from whether `client_inboxes` exists. That table now exists, so the banner and its button vanished **and took every later migration with it**: two were owed (`wallet_tx_types`, `client_contact_name`) and the product offered no way to run either. A control that disappears once its first job is done is not a control. This is exactly the class I had claimed to sweep for hours earlier (*"features with no UI"*) — found one and then shipped one. **Fixed:** a permanent Database card on Engine that always renders, lists every committed migration by key and title, and runs them (all idempotent, so pressing it with nothing outstanding is a no-op). `/operator/engine` now always returns the migration list rather than a single boolean. The amber banner survives as an EXPLANATION only, with no control inside it. **Then audited every control added this session against the same fault** — 13 of them, each checked for what can hide it: Run migrations, Build/reset MBF, the Demo-accounts menu entry, the inline sequence card and the product tour are unconditional; the pick-20 bar, the Top-20 badges, the client-said-this block, no-show/rebook and the legacy-delete rows are conditional on **the thing they act upon existing**, which is correct. Verified `Build it` sits OUTSIDE the `{mbf && …}` wrapper — gated there, you could never build the demo the first time. **A second lesson worth recording:** my automated checker mis-reported three of the thirteen (it reads the nearest preceding JSX condition, not the enclosing one), so each was confirmed by reading the code. A detector that is wrong 23% of the time is how you end up believing a broken thing is fine — the same trap as eighteen unit tests passing on a bypassable gate. Gates: api tsc · **432** vitest · admin builds · doc-lint.
- **26 Jul (✅ PASSES 4, 5 AND 7 CLOSED — all seven done):** finished the three I'd left partial. **Pass 4/5 — every figure on all 80 screens.** Swept for figures COMPUTED in the UI rather than read from the API, because local arithmetic is where a screen and the database diverge. Five sites; four were correctly guarded (the reply/interested rates short-circuit on zero, both Nexus rate renders are a plain ×100). **One real defect:** the client's campaign progress bar divided by `leads_enrolled * 3` — a **hardcoded step count** — so a 5-step sequence read as over-complete (saved only by a `Math.min(100)`) and a 2-step one under-reported. Now uses the campaign's real `steps_count`, which `select('*')` was already returning and the type had thrown away. **Pass 7 — the quiet ones.** Verified: the once-per-lead claim is genuinely atomic (`.is('revealed_at', null)` makes it a conditional update, so exactly one concurrent approve wins) · Stripe webhook retries are idempotent on the **UNIQUE index on `credit_transactions.reference`** (`20260526`), which is what the 23505 catches actually depend on · every `Math.max(...arr)` in live code carries a `, 1` seed so an empty array can't yield `-Infinity` (the two unseeded ones are in the retired `(dashboard)` tree, dead behind the 17-segment redirect) · all five `parseFloat`/`parseInt` money entry points in `stripe.ts` are NaN-guarded before anything moves. **And I caught a false alarm I had introduced an hour earlier:** the new ledger-failure alert would have fired on error **23505** — which is that unique index doing its job, i.e. the lead is already recorded as paid — so every webhook retry would have paged the founder about a double-charge risk that was actually the dedup working. Now excluded. (Self-inflicted on the way: my replacement JSX comment was missing its closing brace and broke the portal build; caught by the build, fixed, rebuilt clean.) **③ The plaintext Postgres password is scrubbed** from `docs/archive/MASTER.md` — and it turned out to be the smoking gun for the migration failure: **the password contains an `@`**, which is exactly what `passwordOf()` was truncating at before this morning's fix. So the "password authentication failed" was never a wrong password — it was my parser cutting a correct one in half. The value remains in git history, so it should still be treated as burned and rotated when Supabase access returns (a history rewrite is the founder's call, not mine). Gates: api tsc + **310** vitest · portal + admin build · doc-lint.
- **25 Jul (🔑 IPv6 SOLVED, now it's the password — and GitHub has removed the Supabase OAuth app entirely):** the pooler fallback worked: *"Tried 19 address(es); last error: **password authentication failed for user postgres**"*. That is a completely different failure from ENETUNREACH — **the database answered**, so the IPv4 route is fixed and this is now a credentials problem. Three things came out of it. ① **Supavisor reports the user as `postgres` even when we connect as `postgres.<ref>`** — it splits the dot into user + tenant — so that name in the error is expected and not evidence of a malformed username; the error text now says so, because it reads like the bug and isn't. ② **My own message was misleading:** it reported *"Tried 19 address(es)"* when the loop had stopped after two (it breaks on a non-routing error, correctly) — it printed the number of candidates GENERATED, not attempted. Fixed, and auth failures now get their own message that says plainly *"the network route is fine, this is a password problem"*, because conflating the two is what sent us chasing IPv6 twice. ③ **`passwordOf()` was truncating at the first `@`** — a generated password containing an unencoded `@` (invalid but very common in pasted connection strings) would come back short, and a short password reads back as *"password authentication failed"*: a parsing bug wearing a credentials bug's clothes. Now greedy to the last `@`, and re-encoded with `encodeURIComponent` when rebuilding. **The unblock:** the founder then reported **GitHub has removed Supabase from their authorised applications too**, so there is now no route into the Supabase dashboard at all — the password cannot be reset there. So the Engine page grows a **"Try a different Postgres password"** box that appears ONLY after the database rejects credentials (never after a network failure — that would send them chasing the wrong problem): used for one run, never stored, never logged, never in the audit row, and still not an arbitrary-SQL hole because the only statements that can run remain the reviewed committed ones. +12 tests. Gates: api tsc + **310** vitest · admin build · doc-lint.
- **25 Jul (🔌 ENETUNREACH — the migration button was never going to work, and it wasn't the SQL):** the founder pressed Vida → Engine → Run it now and got `Failed: 20260725_client_inboxes — connect ENETUNREACH 2a05:d018:cb1:bb00:…:5432`. That address is **IPv6**. Supabase's direct database host (`db.<ref>.supabase.co`) has been **IPv6-only since Jan 2024** unless you pay for the IPv4 add-on, and **Railway containers have no IPv6 egress** — so the connection died before sending a byte. Nothing was wrong with the SQL, the password, or the endpoint: the address is simply unreachable from where we run, which means this button could never have worked and the whole "run migrations from Vida" workaround was resting on a connection that cannot exist. Supabase's own answer is the **pooler (Supavisor)**, which does publish IPv4: `postgres.<ref>@aws-0-<region>.pooler.supabase.com:5432` — port 5432 is SESSION mode, which behaves like a normal connection and runs DDL (6543 is transaction mode, wrong tool for migrations). The one thing not derivable is the **region**, and the founder can't look it up because the dashboard is behind the flagged GitHub account — so `lib/db-connection.ts` generates one candidate per Supabase region (SA/EU first) and the runner **probes for a reachable one before running any SQL** (so a migration is never half-applied across two different connections), then reports which host worked. Two safety details that matter: it **only** retries on routing errors (`ENETUNREACH`/`ENOTFOUND`/timeouts) and **stops dead on a credentials error**, because replaying a bad password against 18 regions is how an account gets locked out; and `safeHost()` means the response and the audit row carry host:port only, never the password. Vida's Engine now says *which* host it connected through plus a hint to set `DATABASE_URL` to the pooler string permanently, so the fallback stops being a fallback. +18 tests (`db-connection.test.ts`). Gates: api tsc + **298** vitest · admin build · doc-lint.

Both rows corrected in the inventory rather than left to mislead the next session. **The genuine gaps, now shipped —** #317: ① **`charge.dispute.closed` was unhandled**, so a dispute WE WON left the client permanently short (we keep the cash Stripe returns AND their wallet stays clawed back — us taking money for nothing) → restores on `won` only, idempotent on `refund_restore_<id>`, and note **`type:'adjustment'` is NOT in `credit_transactions`' CHECK constraint**: the first draft used it, which would have thrown, 500'd the webhook and had Stripe retry forever while the client was never restored (`manual_grant` is the valid type; pinned in a new `refund-policy.test.ts` so it can't come back); ② **the money stopped but the WORK didn't** — a clawed-back wallet blocks the next $4 approval, yet every enrollment already mid-sequence kept emailing prospects in a refunded client's name, burning our sending reputation for someone who had just taken their money back → active campaigns now pause on refund/dispute, reversibly, with an alert. #290: ③ **`error_events` may never have existed in prod** — its migration was written to be run by hand in the Supabase editor the founder has been locked out of since, and the handler swallows the insert failure by design, so capture may have been quietly degraded the whole time → the migration is now in `pending-migrations.ts`, runnable from **Vida → Engine → Run it now**; ④ **`uncaughtException` / `unhandledRejection` only `console.error`'d**, so the crash class — the most serious kind — was the *only* one that never alerted → both now record + alert through the same hourly throttle. - **25 Jul (🔎 PRE-MERGE AUDIT of the launch path — five defects, two of them shipping-broken):** founder: *"audit this before I merge."* Right call — the audit found five, run against the built code rather than from memory. **① Would have broken the entire cockpit:** `/operator/cockpit` selected `daily_send_limit` as a column, and **there is no such column** — the cap lives in `settings.daily_send_limit` JSONB (read by the send cron in `internal.ts` and the #511 auto-tune). - **25 Jul (🚀 THE LAUNCH PATH — the shell was live, the features were not; built end to end, no SQL):** we walked the flow instead of building, and the founder named the real problem: *"the old portal, I used to create an ICP via a conversation with FIGSY… then generate people, assign them to a campaign, be suggested a campaign, edit a sequence, send a test email and hit go. Right now in Vida I can't see this process. The shell is here but the features are not."* Root cause is the same auth wall as the cockpit: every one of those endpoints is gated by `requireAuth` (a **client** Bearer JWT), and the admin app proxies with `x-admin-key` and **no client session** — so the operator could look at a campaign and never propose, fill, preview, test or run one. Built the operator twins plus the client's half (#520–#537), all on tables that already exist, so **none of it was blocked on the Supabase lockout**. **Vida** now runs the path end to end: **ICP by conversation** (`POST /operator/icp/chat`, seeded with the client's current active ICP so it refines rather than restarts; the field form stays as the precise-edit fallback) → **multi-select the people** (`GET /operator/people` — whole pool, masked emails stay masked, shows who is already enrolled) → **assign the picked ones** (`POST /operator/campaign/:id/assign`, `prepaid` so it never re-charges) → **Vida proposes the campaign, the operator approves** (`/campaign/suggest` → `/campaign/save`; deterministic when there's no LLM key — never invents, never fails the step) → **edit it** (name · the brief every email is written from · daily cap) with an **Auto-Pilot / Co-Pilot** choice (Co-Pilot writes `approve_before_send`; new campaigns start there on purpose) → **Vida drafts the sequence** against a real top-scoring lead then de-tokenises it into a template (`/sequence/suggest`) → **preview it as the prospect reads it** (`GET /operator/sequence/:id/preview`) → **email yourself a test** (`/campaign/:id/test`, subject-prefixed `[TEST · client]` — the last gate before a real prospect) → **Run it** → **see who's in it** and where each of them is (`/campaign/:id/enrollments`). Plus the **bell** (`GET /operator/alerts`, derived live from real rows — new client whose first ICP waits on us · ICP revised under a live campaign · replies to answer) and **"Ask them for these"**, which now genuinely reaches the client: `POST /operator/ask` writes into their own Milla thread, because the conversation IS the surface and no new table was needed. **Milla's half:** the thread **persists across visits** (it started blank every time, so an ask could never be seen, let alone answered), the client **revises their ICP by conversation** and it **goes live** — founder-locked, no gate on their own change — while alerting us because anyone already enrolled was picked against the OLD profile (`POST /icps/revise`), and they **see the sequence read-only** on My campaign. The top-left brand is now a link home in both consoles. **Two honesty fixes, both of which would have read as working:** assign counted successful `autoEnrollLead` calls, but that function returns `void` and bails silently on no verified email / suppression / a CRM match / exhausted FIGSY credits / an unconfigured sender — it would have reported "12 added" when 3 were, so the count now comes from `figsy_enrollments` and names what didn't make it; and because `autoEnrollLead` enrols into the client's most recent **active** campaign (it takes no campaign id), assigning to a paused campaign did nothing quietly — it now refuses with *"'X' isn't running — hit Run it first."* The `{{token}}` contract was extracted to `lib/sequence-tokens.ts` (+12 tests) so the suggest and preview halves can't drift into a literal `{{first_name}}` reaching a prospect. Gates: api tsc + **241** vitest (+12) · portal + admin build · doc-lint. **No migration.** Deferred by the founder: refining the approval gates.

- **24 Jul (🔎 OPUS VERIFY of the console PR — caught + fixed a money-for-nothing regression):** verify pass over the overnight Sonnet build (#1146). The nav/declutter work and the Vida escape fix hold up — links, API contract (`/figsy/campaigns` column names), the middleware rewrite (no fetch() breakage, no redirect loop, `/vida/*` correctly excluded) all check out. **But the PR shipped a real, money-losing regression.** Removing the client's self-serve FIGSY console took away the ONLY UI in the entire system that could create a campaign: `POST /figsy/campaigns` is client-JWT-only, the operator/admin side has **no** campaign-create endpoint at all, and nothing auto-creates one (the only other inserts are seed/demo + clone/apply-sequence, which need an existing campaign). Meanwhile `approveLead` charges the flat $4 and then calls `autoEnrollLead(..., {force:true})`, which **bails silently** on `if (!campaign) return` — and the call is `.catch(() => {})`. Net: a client pays $99, approves a lead, is **debited $4, gets no enrolment, no outreach, no error and no refund** — and no console could fix it. **Fixed three ways:** ① `approveLead` now fail-closes *before* the charge — no active campaign ⟹ status `no_campaign`, wallet untouched, claim released, founder alerted (`sends_stalled`); it never takes money for work it can't run (the same principle as the insufficient-funds gate). ② New operator endpoint `POST /operator/campaign/start` (admin-key gated, audited as `start_campaign`, idempotent, creates **ACTIVE** — the table default is `draft`, which would leave the client just as blocked) + a "Start campaign" button on the Vida per-client page that appears exactly when a client has no active campaign, explaining why approvals are blocked. ③ Both `/leads/:id/approve` and `/reveal` return a 409 `no_campaign`, and Milla translates the known error CODES into plain English (the portal's api helper surfaces `error`, not `message`, so a client would otherwise have literally read "no_campaign"). +2 regression tests (no charge, no reversal, claim released) — the test harness's table-agnostic mock was also made table-aware so the guard is actually exercised. Gates: api tsc + **229** vitest (+2) · portal + admin build clean · doc-lint green. No migration.

Recommendation: in the managed model no lead should enroll/charge before the client's 👍 — gate the auto/self-serve enroll paths off for managed clients (or add a per-(client,lead) "work-charged-once" marker both paths honour). briefly considered an exit) — and pivoted the go-to-market, NOT the code: **we run the outbound as a done-for-you concierge on the existing FIGSY engine (~70% reuse), branded Milla&Vida.** Locked model: **FIGSY = the engine** (no longer sold as a standalone product) · **Vida = the OPERATOR console** — WE run the full loop for a client (ICP → source → draft → approve → send → triage → book); the current admin "nervous system" folds in as Vida's **top-right dropdown**, and a **client-picker** lets one operator run any client · **Milla = the CLIENT portal** — the client reviews masked leads and clicks **👍 approve / ✕ pass**, plus concierge chat, meetings, reports (react-only) · **Nexus = the per-client private learning brain** (learns on that client's results only, never shared across clients). **Money (unchanged spine, new trigger): the $4 fires ONLY on the client's 👍 approve** — $1 reveal + $3 work — **reviewing is free**, dead-email auto-refund, **prepaid credits ("we never chase payments")**. **Approve-then-reveal is the trust gate:** leads are shown **masked (no email) until the client approves**, so operator-controlled reveal can never run up a client's bill — the client's 👍 authorises the reveal. **Glide path (no migration ever):** managed → co-pilot → self-serve — only *who clicks approve* changes (us → us+them → them); the same product slides along it. **Client Zero:** we are our own first managed client — our own outreach IS the live demo. **Trade as Milla&Vida** (K.I.N.D stays the registered company); keep the current site framework ("make it look like a million bucks — don't walk away from the build"). **Build order locked: Website → Vida (the real part) → Milla, ~2 weeks, aligned to payday.** The **9-Jul→22-Jul 5-block roadmap is SUPERSEDED** by this 14-day build — its unshipped items stay in the inventory (no IDs lost); Built (Opus): googleapis re-added (lazy-load kept) · **#368** HMAC-signed OAuth state (10-min TTL, timingSafeEqual) · per-lead 90-day signed **booking token** = the auth for two public rate-limited routes (`/calendar/public/:token/slots|book`) · shared `performBooking` (freebusy race re-check → real Google event + Meet link → `calendar_bookings` → `meeting_booked` outcome → campaign KPI) with one-active-booking-per-lead 409 dedup · public portal page **`/book/<token>`** (prospect's own timezone, honest error states) · FIGSY now drops the per-lead tokenised link into sequences when the calendar is connected (falls back to static `booking_url`) · slots drawn in the CALENDAR's timezone not server UTC · Google auth failure flips `calendar_booking_enabled` off honestly. **Fable verify caught 3 pre-merge: (F1)** the booking page used React-19 `use(params)` on React 18 → would crash for every prospect — fixed to Next-14 params; **(F2)** token-type confusion — an emailed booking token also passed OAuth-state verification, reopening #368 via side door → type claims added + cross-replay tests; **(F3)** public book accepted ANY start/end (a prospect could create a 10-hour 3am event) → requested slot must now match a server-offered slot. Gates: api tsc + 185/185 · portal tsc + build. Migration `20260722_calendar_booking_live.sql` (idempotent). #361 #368 → 🩷. **Walk needs 🧍: run the migration + `railway up` api/portal + set `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`/`GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` on @kind/api (Google Cloud OAuth consent + web credentials) — feature deploys safely dormant until set.** Board 🟢90·🩷148·🟣1·🟡18·🔴218·⏸5·Σ480, lint green. _Code (api+portal) + docs._
